----- probe_curve.py -----
How early does the model know its answer? Probe the answer distribution as thinking grows.

For each question this samples full rollouts, then cuts each rollout's thinking after k tokens
(k = 0, stride, 2*stride, ...), closes it with "\n</think>\n\nFinal answer:" (the newline only
when the cut does not already end with one, as Qwen3 writes it) and reads the
model's probability of every candidate answer by teacher forcing -- no sampling. The rollout
is read once and every cut is scored as a short segment attached to it, so a cut costs only
its closing tokens. The curve p(gold | first k thinking tokens) shows how much of the thinking comes
after the answer is already settled.

Drop this file at the root of ThinkingCap-Test (next to train.py) and run:

    python utils/train/prepare_data.py                          # writes data/val.jsonl
    python probe_curve.py --out runs/probe --per-source 50      # GSM8K, Skywork, CSQA, ARC
    python probe_curve.py --out runs/probe --summarize-only     # re-read the results

Phase 1 generates with vLLM (the eval's sampling settings) in a child process, so the GPU is
free again for phase 2, which scores with Transformers. Both write to --out, and phase 1 is
skipped when its rollouts already exist.

Options:
  parser.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
  parser.add_argument("--adapter", help="sample from this LoRA on top of --model (on-policy rollouts)")
  parser.add_argument("--data", default="data/val.jsonl")
  parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--per-source", type=int, default=50, help="questions per dataset")
  parser.add_argument("--sources", help="comma-separated datasets to keep (default: all)")
  parser.add_argument("--samples", type=int, default=4, help="rollouts per question")
  parser.add_argument("--max-tokens", type=int, default=4096, help="token cap, as the evaluation's (4,096)")
  parser.add_argument("--code-max-tokens", type=int, default=8192, help="token cap for code, as MBPP+'s (8,192)")
  parser.add_argument("--stride", type=int, default=16, help="thinking tokens between probes; 1 probes every marginal token")
  parser.add_argument("--batch-tokens", type=int, default=32768, help="padded tokens per scoring forward pass; lower it if memory runs out")
  parser.add_argument("--threshold", type=float, default=0.9, help="p(gold) that counts as 'settled' in the summary")
  parser.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16", help="float16 on a T4")
  parser.add_argument("--attention-backend", default=None, help="vLLM attention backend; TRITON_ATTN on a T4, where FlashInfer prefill fails")
  parser.add_argument("--chunk", type=int, default=0, help="sample this many questions at a time and append each chunk to rollouts.jsonl.partial, " "resuming from it after a disconnect (0: one batch, saved at the end)")
  parser.add_argument("--exclude-rollouts", nargs="*", default=[], help="chunk mode: skip the questions already in this rollouts file")
  parser.add_argument("--max-questions", type=int, default=0, help="chunk mode: stop after this many questions")
  parser.add_argument("--summarize-only", action="store_true")
  parser.add_argument("--generate-only", action="store_true", help=argparse.SUPPRESS)

----- prune_traces.py -----
Shorten the model's own correct rollouts into traces it still gets right: SFT targets.

For each correct rollout from probe_curve.py:

  1. Split the thinking into steps (paragraphs) and score p(gold) after every step, the same
     teacher-forced probe probe_curve.py uses.
  2. Cut everything after the step from which p(gold) stays above --threshold: the model
     already knew the answer.
  3. Before that point, try deleting the steps that did not raise p(gold) ("flat" steps).
     A deletion is kept only if p(gold) of the *whole edited trace* stays above the
     threshold, because a step can matter without raising p(gold) by itself (setting up a
     variable). Flat steps are tested in groups and the groups split on failure, so a
     trace with many removable steps costs a few forward passes, not one per step.
  4. Let the model answer naturally from the edited thinking (no forced "Final answer:")
     --check-samples times. The trace is kept only if every answer is graded correct.

--rule mvt replaces step 2's fixed threshold with the Marginal Value Theorem (Charnov 1976):
thinking is foraging for certainty, g(t) is p(gold) after t thinking tokens (its running
maximum, the gain secured so far), and the model should leave the patch at the first t where
the best gain rate still available, (g(t') - g(t)) / (t' - t) for any later t', drops to the
average rate g(t) / (t + c) at every later point. The travel cost c (--travel) is the answer tokens that follow
anyway: a larger c means staying longer. Step 3 then keeps a deletion if p(gold) stays at the
level the cut reached (or the threshold, whichever is lower).

--rule marginal is the marginal token analysis at the level of --unit: delete each unit alone
and measure the p(gold) it costs; units costing at most --flat are deleted (in groups that
split on failure, as in step 3) as long as the whole edited trace keeps p(gold) within --flat
of the full trace. No truncation.

--unit picks what a "step" is: paragraph (default), sentence, or tokensN (fixed batches of N
thinking tokens, e.g. tokens32).

Code, tool-call and instruction rollouts (typed.OWN_ANSWER) have no short gold answer: their
"p(gold)" is p(the rollout's own correct answer | thinking), a per-token geometric mean.

--checker vllm runs every natural-answer check of the run in one vLLM batch (check_vllm.py),
which long code and instruction answers need; hf checks one trace at a time.

Per question it keeps the shortest accepted completion (thinking + answer, as the evaluation
counts it). Questions whose p(gold) never settles keep their shortest correct original
rollout, so training does not teach "always stop early" on the hard ones.

    python probe_curve.py --out runs/probe            # phase 1 writes runs/probe/rollouts.jsonl
    python prune_traces.py --rollouts runs/probe/rollouts.jsonl --out runs/prune
    python sft.py --data runs/prune/sft.jsonl --out adapters/pruned-sft

Options:
  parser.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
  parser.add_argument("--rollouts", required=True, help="rollouts.jsonl from probe_curve.py")
  parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--rule", choices=("settle", "mvt", "marginal"), default="settle", help="settle: cut where p(gold) reaches --threshold and stays; " "mvt: cut where the marginal gain drops to the average rate (Charnov)")
  parser.add_argument("--travel", type=float, default=256, help="mvt travel cost c in tokens: the answer that follows anyway; larger stays longer")
  parser.add_argument("--adapter", help="LoRA to merge into --model before scoring and checking")
  parser.add_argument("--sources", type=lambda text: set(text.split(",")), default=set(), help="only these datasets' rollouts (default: all)")
  parser.add_argument("--easy-tokens", type=int, default=0, help="keep only questions with a correct rollout at most this long (0: all)")
  parser.add_argument("--unit", default="paragraph", help="paragraph, sentence, or tokensN (e.g. tokens32)")
  parser.add_argument("--checker", choices=("hf", "vllm"), default="hf")
  parser.add_argument("--full-sources", type=lambda text: set(text.split(",")), default=set(), help="datasets kept whole (shortest correct rollout, no cut), e.g. gsm8k")
  parser.add_argument("--threshold", type=float, default=0.9, help="p(gold) a kept trace must reach")
  parser.add_argument("--flat", type=float, default=0.02, help="a step whose p(gold) gain over the best earlier point is at most this is 'flat'")
  parser.add_argument("--min-steps", type=int, default=1, help="never prune the thinking below this")
  parser.add_argument("--margin", type=int, default=0, help="steps kept after the settle point: p(gold) can settle before a small model " "has done the work its sampled answer needs")
  parser.add_argument("--check-samples", type=int, default=2, help="natural answers that must all be correct; 0 skips the check")
  parser.add_argument("--answer-tokens", type=int, default=512, help="cap on a natural answer")
  parser.add_argument("--batch-tokens", type=int, default=32768)
  parser.add_argument("--no-keep-unsettled", dest="keep_unsettled", action="store_false")
  parser.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16")

----- rl_train.py -----
RL stage: reward correct answers that are as short as the model's own shortest correct one,
and push length pressure onto the thinking tokens that carry no information.

Each step samples --group replies to each of --questions training questions with the current
LoRA, then takes one policy-gradient step (GRPO-style group advantages, on-policy):

  reward    a correct reply gets 1 - alpha * |len - L*| / L*, a wrong or cut-off one
            -alpha * |len - L*| / L*, where L* is the length of the shortest correct reply in its
            group, or the group mean when none is correct (ShorterBetter's "sample optimal
            length"): every question sets its own budget.
  advantage reward minus the group mean, over the group's standard deviation.
  per token on correct replies, thinking tokens marked redundant get an extra -lambda. Two
            markers: "confidence" (tokens the policy itself found unlikely, in the bottom
            --redundant-quantile of the reply: exploratory filler, as in TokenProbe), or
            "settle" (tokens after the point where p(correct answer) settled, read with
            probe_curve.probe_scores: the model already knew).
  TokenProbe ("On the Token Value Inequality in Efficient Reasoning", --redundant tokenprobe-rs or
            tokenprobe-kl): each reply's token log-probs are z-scored (NormLP); the K best
            non-overlapping W-token windows by summed NormLP are "core", the rest redundant.
            Redundant tokens are pressed only when the reply is over budget L = core_len * scale,
            scale annealed from --scale-start to --scale-end.
              rs: A_t += beta * (alpha_core * NormLP_t [core] - lam * max(0, T - L) / T [redundant])
              kl: core tokens (and in-budget replies) are anchored to the base model with a KL
                  term beta_kl; over-budget redundant tokens lose the anchor and get
                  -gamma_t * log pi with gamma_t = lam_decay * (T - L) / T.
            Wrong replies get the plain update (plus the KL anchor in kl mode).

Stability knobs (DLER: the penalty matters less than stable training): --lr-end with
--restarts gives a cosine decay that restarts --restarts times; --updates splits each step's
groups into that many optimizer steps; --entropy-coef adds a bonus on the policy's mean token
entropy, which is logged every step so a collapse (the model no longer exploring) shows.

AdaptThink (--no-think-share): that share of each group starts with an empty
"<think>

</think>

", the prefix Qwen3's own non-thinking mode uses, as part of the reply.
The policy is trained on those prefix tokens too, so it learns when skipping the thinking still
gives a correct answer; that reply is the group's shortest, so the length reward favours it
whenever it is right. --no-think-bonus adds AdaptThink's delta to correct no-think replies.

Start from the SFT adapter (--init-adapter) so the model already knows how to stop.

    python rl_train.py --init-adapter adapters/pruned_v2 --out adapters/rl --steps 6
    python evaluate.py --adapter adapters/rl --out runs/rl

Options:
  parser.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
  parser.add_argument("--init-adapter", help="LoRA to continue from (the SFT warm start)")
  parser.add_argument("--data", default="data/train.jsonl")
  parser.add_argument("--sources", default="gsm8k,skywork,arc_easy,commonsense_qa")
  parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--steps", type=int, default=6)
  parser.add_argument("--questions", type=int, default=32, help="questions per step")
  parser.add_argument("--group", type=int, default=8, help="replies per question")
  parser.add_argument("--max-tokens", type=int, default=2048)
  parser.add_argument("--alpha", type=float, default=0.5, help="weight of the distance to L*")
  parser.add_argument("--lam", type=float, default=0.1, help="extra penalty per redundant token")
  parser.add_argument("--redundant", default="confidence", choices=["confidence", "settle", "tokenprobe-rs", "tokenprobe-kl", "none"])
  parser.add_argument("--window", type=int, default=10, help="TokenProbe core window W")
  parser.add_argument("--windows", type=int, default=10, help="TokenProbe core windows K")
  parser.add_argument("--scale-start", type=float, default=3.0, help="budget = core_len * scale")
  parser.add_argument("--scale-end", type=float, default=1.5)
  parser.add_argument("--rs-beta", type=float, default=1.0)
  parser.add_argument("--rs-alpha", type=float, default=0.05)
  parser.add_argument("--rs-lam", type=float, default=0.08)
  parser.add_argument("--kl-beta", type=float, default=5e-3)
  parser.add_argument("--lam-decay", type=float, default=0.2)
  parser.add_argument("--redundant-quantile", type=float, default=0.2)
  parser.add_argument("--settle-threshold", type=float, default=0.9)
  parser.add_argument("--lr", type=float, default=2e-5)
  parser.add_argument("--lr-end", type=float, help="cosine-decay the lr to this (default: constant)")
  parser.add_argument("--restarts", type=int, default=1, help="cosine cycles over the run (warm restarts)")
  parser.add_argument("--updates", type=int, default=1, help="optimizer steps per sampling step")
  parser.add_argument("--entropy-coef", type=float, default=0.0, help="bonus on mean token entropy")
  parser.add_argument("--no-think-share", type=float, default=0.0, help="share of each group sampled with an empty thinking block (AdaptThink)")
  parser.add_argument("--no-think-bonus", type=float, default=0.0)
  parser.add_argument("--batch-tokens", type=int, default=4096, help="padded tokens per training forward pass")
  parser.add_argument("--rank", type=int, default=16, help="only used without --init-adapter")
  parser.add_argument("--backend", choices=["vllm", "hf"], default="vllm")
  parser.add_argument("--attention-backend", default=None, help="vLLM backend; TRITON_ATTN on a T4")
  parser.add_argument("--gpu-memory", type=float, default=0.35, help="vLLM share of the GPU")
  parser.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16")
  parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)
  parser.add_argument("--reward", choices=["shorter", "arora"], default="shorter", help="arora: correct x (1 - alpha_q * sigmoid(z)), z = the reply's thinking length " "standardized among the group's correct replies; wrong or cut off = 0")
  parser.add_argument("--adaptive", action="store_true", help="arora: alpha_q = alpha x the group's solve rate (ALP): hard questions keep their thinking")
  parser.add_argument("--advantage", choices=["group", "batch"], default="group", help="batch: reward minus the group mean, over the whole batch's spread (DLER), not each group's")
  parser.add_argument("--kl-coef", type=float, default=0.0, help="k3 KL to the model without the LoRA, per token")
  parser.add_argument("--grader", choices=["task", "typed"], default="task", help="typed: typed.is_correct (math, multiple choice, code tests, tools, instructions)")
  parser.add_argument("--offset", type=int, default=0, help="steps already done in earlier blocks: question slice and seed continue from there")
  parser.add_argument("--save-every", type=int, default=0, help="also keep the LoRA every N steps (out/step-N)")
  parser.add_argument("--eval-only", action="store_true", help="no training: score --init-adapter (or the base model) on the first --questions of --data")

----- select_shortest.py -----
Shortest-of-N self-distillation data: for each question, the shortest of the model's own correct replies.

Nothing is cut or edited: every target is a reply the model actually wrote and that the
evaluation's graders accept, so training only shifts the model toward its own brief, correct
behaviour. With N samples per question the shortest correct reply is typically well under the
mean length; with N = 2 (our "whole" runs) it barely differs.

    python select_shortest.py --rollouts runs/bon/probe/rollouts.jsonl --out runs/bon/select \
        --easy-tokens gsm8k_all=700

Writes sft.jsonl (sft.py's format) and summary.txt: per source, how many questions have a
correct reply, the mean length of all replies, of the correct ones and of the kept ones.

Options:
  parser.add_argument("--rollouts", required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--easy-tokens", nargs="*", default=[], help="source=N: keep that source's question only if its shortest correct reply is at most N " "tokens (e.g. GSM8K down to SVAMP's difficulty)")
  parser.add_argument("--min-saved", type=float, default=0.0, help="keep a question only if its shortest correct reply is at least this share " "shorter than its mean reply (a clear brevity signal)")

----- sft.py -----
LoRA fine-tune Qwen3-0.6B on the pruned traces from prune_traces.py.

The loss covers the completion only (thinking + answer), never the prompt. The adapter is
saved in .safetensors and loads straight into the evaluation:

    python sft.py --data runs/prune/sft.jsonl --out adapters/pruned-sft
    python evaluate.py --out runs/pruned-sft --adapter adapters/pruned-sft
    python compare.py runs/base runs/pruned-sft

Every --save-every optimizer steps it also writes a checkpoint, giving several points on the
length/accuracy curve.

Options:
  parser.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
  parser.add_argument("--init-only", action="store_true", help="save a fresh, untrained LoRA and stop")
  parser.add_argument("--data", default="", help="sft.jsonl from prune_traces.py")
  parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--epochs", type=int, default=2)
  parser.add_argument("--lr", type=float, default=1e-4)
  parser.add_argument("--rank", type=int, default=16)
  parser.add_argument("--accum", type=int, default=16, help="sequences per optimizer step")
  parser.add_argument("--max-len", type=int, default=4096)
  parser.add_argument("--save-every", type=int, default=0, help="optimizer steps; 0 saves only at the end")
  parser.add_argument("--log-every", type=int, default=10)
  parser.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16", help="float16 on a T4")
  parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)
  parser.add_argument("--max-per-source", type=int, default=0, help="cap rows per dataset; 0 keeps all")
  parser.add_argument("--repeat", nargs="*", default=[], help="dataset:times, e.g. gsm8k:2, after the cap")
  parser.add_argument("--init-adapter", help="start from this LoRA instead of a fresh one")

----- steer.py -----
Steering vector for concise thinking, stored in the weights as a plain bias (no custom code).

ASC-style: sample --group replies to each question with the model (optionally with a LoRA),
pair each question's shortest correct reply with its longest correct one (at least --ratio
times longer), and take the mean difference of the residual stream after layer --layer over
the thinking tokens: v = mean(concise) - mean(verbose).

The vector goes into the weights through Qwen3's `attention_bias` flag. A bias b on v_proj adds
the same vector to every value, so, since attention weights sum to one, every position's
attention output gains W_o R b (R repeats each key-value head over its query heads). Solving
W_o R b = strength * v puts exactly strength * v into the residual stream at that layer, at
every position, in a model that standard Transformers and vLLM load as is. All other biases are
zero. A LoRA trained on the base model still applies on top (--adapter at evaluation).

    python steer.py --out steered/raw --strengths 0.5,1,2
    python steer.py --adapter adapters/rl_kl --out steered/rl --strengths 0.5,1,2
    python rl_train.py --eval-only --model steered/raw/s1 --data data/val.jsonl --out runs/held/steer1

Options:
  parser.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
  parser.add_argument("--adapter", help="LoRA active while sampling and reading activations")
  parser.add_argument("--data", default="data/train.jsonl")
  parser.add_argument("--sources", default="gsm8k,skywork,arc_easy,commonsense_qa")
  parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--questions", type=int, default=192)
  parser.add_argument("--group", type=int, default=8)
  parser.add_argument("--max-tokens", type=int, default=2048)
  parser.add_argument("--ratio", type=float, default=1.5, help="verbose reply at least this much longer")
  parser.add_argument("--layer", type=int, help="decoder layer that receives the vector (default: middle)")
  parser.add_argument("--strengths", default="0.5,1,2")
  parser.add_argument("--slice-seed", type=int, default=99, help="question slice, apart from the RL runs'")
  parser.add_argument("--backend", choices=["vllm", "hf"], default="vllm")
  parser.add_argument("--attention-backend", default=None)
  parser.add_argument("--gpu-memory", type=float, default=0.5)
  parser.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16")
  parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)

----- steer_dirs.py -----
Three "be brief" directions in the model's internal state, found from its own replies, kept small.

The raw model's shortest-of-8 rollouts (runs/bon/probe) hold, for the same question, short and
long correct replies. We read the residual stream after every layer over the first --window
thinking tokens (where the model "decides" how much to think, before long replies start
checking themselves), then build, per layer and per kind of task:

  A  mean difference: mean over questions of (shortest correct - longest correct)
  B  within-question trend: the direction that grows as correct replies get shorter, over every
     correct reply of the question (centred per question, against log length), minus its part
     along the "gives up" direction (wrong replies - correct replies of the same question)
  C  learned: one vector added at one layer, trained (everything else frozen) so the model
     prefers its own shortest correct reply over its longest (DPO against the unpushed model,
     NLL anchor on the short reply)

The push goes into the weights as a v_proj bias (steer.py), so standard Transformers and vLLM
load the model as is. Questions are split by id: directions use the "fit" ones, the layer
filter and the fresh rollouts use the "held" ones.

    python steer_dirs.py states --rollouts runs/bon/probe/rollouts.jsonl --out runs/steer
    python steer_dirs.py directions --out runs/steer
    python steer_dirs.py learn --out runs/steer --layer 14
    python steer_dirs.py build --out runs/steer --kind A --layer 14 --strength 2 --target steered/A-14-2
    python steer_dirs.py check --model steered/A-14-2
    python steer_dirs.py held --out runs/steer --data data/bon.jsonl --per-source 25
    python steer_dirs.py compare --out runs/steer --runs runs/steer/roll-A runs/steer/roll-B

Options:
  parser.add_argument("command", choices=("states", "directions", "learn", "build", "check", "vllm_logprobs", "held", "compare"))
  parser.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
  parser.add_argument("--raw", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
  parser.add_argument("--rollouts", default="runs/bon/probe/rollouts.jsonl")
  parser.add_argument("--data", default="data/bon.jsonl")
  parser.add_argument("--out", default="runs/steer")
  parser.add_argument("--per-kind", type=int, default=200, help="states: questions per kind of task")
  parser.add_argument("--all-replies", action="store_true", help="states: every finished reply, not 4 per question")
  parser.add_argument("--window", type=int, default=64, help="states: first thinking tokens averaged")
  parser.add_argument("--ratio", type=float, default=1.5, help="longest correct at least this much longer")
  parser.add_argument("--top", type=int, default=2, help="directions: candidate layers kept")
  parser.add_argument("--layer", type=int)
  parser.add_argument("--kind", choices=("A", "B", "C"))
  parser.add_argument("--strength", type=float, default=1.0)
  parser.add_argument("--target")
  parser.add_argument("--pairs", type=int, default=200, help="learn: pairs, balanced over kinds")
  parser.add_argument("--max-len", type=int, default=5000, help="learn: longest prompt + reply")
  parser.add_argument("--lr", type=float, default=0.05, help="learn: step per coordinate, in units of |A|/sqrt(d)")
  parser.add_argument("--beta", type=float, default=0.1)
  parser.add_argument("--nll", type=float, default=1.0)
  parser.add_argument("--batch", type=int, default=8)
  parser.add_argument("--ids")
  parser.add_argument("--per-source", type=int, default=25, help="held: fresh questions per source")
  parser.add_argument("--runs", nargs="*", default=[])

----- subset_questions.py -----
Write a smaller evaluation selection: the first N questions of every benchmark.

The full evaluation (2,759 questions x 8 samples) takes days on a free T4. This keeps the
first N questions of each benchmark in the order utils/eval/benchmarks.py shuffled them,
the same way its smoke selection does, with their seed_index unchanged, so a question
draws the generations it would draw in a full run. Score the base model and your
checkpoint on the same subset; the numbers are a development signal, not the leaderboard.

    python utils/eval/benchmarks.py                              # writes questions/
    python subset_questions.py --per-dataset 40 --out questions_40
    python evaluate.py --questions questions_40 --samples 2 --out runs/base

With --seed, the N questions per benchmark are drawn at random instead (seeded, so the same set
can be rebuilt), leaving out every question of --exclude: a fresh test set nothing was tuned on.

    python subset_questions.py --per-dataset 40 --seed 20261022 --exclude questions_40 --out questions_fresh

Options:
  parser.add_argument("--questions", default="questions", help="directory from benchmarks.py")
  parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--per-dataset", type=int, required=True)
  parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, help="draw at random with this seed instead of taking the first N")
  parser.add_argument("--exclude", nargs="*", default=[], help="selection directories whose questions are never drawn")

----- sweep_eval.py -----
Score several LoRA checkpoints in one vLLM engine, with evaluate.py's questions, seeds, sampling and graders.

evaluate.py loads the engine for every checkpoint and scores it alone. Here the engine loads
once with every adapter registered, and all checkpoints' requests share its batches, so a sweep
of N checkpoints costs about one evaluation. Each checkpoint gets its own --out directory with
evaluate.py's records.jsonl and results.json, which compare.py reads as usual.

Generations are statistically equivalent to evaluate.py's, not bit-identical: vLLM's output at a
fixed seed depends on what shares the batch. Use it for quick sweeps; score finalists with
evaluate.py.

    python sweep_eval.py --questions questions_40 --samples 2 \
        --runs runs/a=adapters/x/step-10 runs/b=adapters/x/step-20

Options:
  parser.add_argument("--questions", default="questions_40")
  parser.add_argument("--samples", type=int, default=2)
  parser.add_argument("--runs", nargs="+", required=True, help="out_dir=adapter_dir, one per checkpoint")

----- track.py -----
Run the MVT-cut and fading-worked-examples track end to end, one idempotent stage at a time.

Every stage skips work whose output already exists, so a cell can be rerun after a disconnect.
Run from the ThinkingCap-Test root, with this repo's scripts copied next to evaluate.py:

    python track.py setup                 # training data, benchmark questions, 240-question subset
    python track.py base                  # the raw model on the subset (the reference)
    python track.py rollouts              # the model's own replies to 450 training questions
    python track.py mvt --travel 256      # experiment 1: MVT cut -> SFT -> evaluate
    python track.py report                # where MVT cuts vs the settle rule, for many travel costs
    python track.py fade --rounds 2       # experiment 2: fading paragraphs + words, stops past -3 points
    python track.py fade --rounds 4 --word-scorer marginal   # experiment 3: words scored by our own model
    python track.py scores                # every evaluated run against the base

Options:
  parser.add_argument("stage", choices=("setup", "base", "rollouts", "mvt", "report", "fade", "plan", "recheck", "mix", "full", "grid_data", "grid", "gridtable", "combine", "merge", "fullbest", "stats", "bon", "bon_dpo", "code_round", "chain", "repair", "rlcal", "audit", "balanced", "full2", "clean", "confirm", "fresh", "peek", "s1log", "end", "diag", "steer_read", "steer", "dpo_round", "scores"))
  parser.add_argument("--travel", type=float, default=256)
  parser.add_argument("--rounds", type=int, default=5)
  parser.add_argument("--order", default="gain")
  parser.add_argument("--floor-travel", type=float)
  parser.add_argument("--word-step", type=float, default=0.1, help="fading: words compacted per round; 0 = paragraphs only")
  parser.add_argument("--tag", default="", help="suffix for the fading run's directories")
  parser.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16")
  parser.add_argument("--adapter", help="recheck: the adapter to score; none scores the base")
  parser.add_argument("--ignore-limit", action="store_true", help="fade: keep going past the -3-point limit")
  parser.add_argument("--mc", help="mix: sft.jsonl giving the non-math rows")
  parser.add_argument("--math", help="mix: sft.jsonl giving the GSM8K rows")
  parser.add_argument("--name", help="mix: run name")
  parser.add_argument("--type", choices=("math", "code", "tool", "text", "choice"), help="grid: the kind of data trained on")
  parser.add_argument("--method", choices=("whole", "mvt", "marginal", "fade"), help="grid: how its traces are shortened")
  parser.add_argument("--unit", default="paragraph", help="grid: paragraph, sentence or tokensN")
  parser.add_argument("--per-source", type=int, default=250, help="grid_data: questions per kind")
  parser.add_argument("--start", help="dpo_round: the adapter the round starts from (policy and reference)")
  parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=1, help="dpo_round: question draw (bon used seed 0)")
  parser.add_argument("--steps", type=int, default=150, help="dpo_round: DPO steps; checkpoints every 25")
  parser.add_argument("--inputs", nargs="*", default=[], help="combine: sft.jsonl[:source[:source...]] files whose rows (of those sources) are pooled")
  parser.add_argument("--margin", type=float, default=-2.5, help="fullbest: lowest 4-sample accuracy change (points) a candidate may have")
  parser.add_argument("--plan-file", default="plan.txt", help="plan: the queue this runtime works through")
  parser.add_argument("--wait-hours", type=float, default=0, help="plan: when the queue is empty, keep polling the branch this long; a running cell " "also keeps Colab from disconnecting an idle runtime and wiping its disk")
  parser.add_argument("--samples", type=int, default=4, help="recheck: samples per question")
  parser.add_argument("--no-shorten", default="", help="bon_dpo: sources with guard pairs only")
  parser.add_argument("--eval-samples", type=int, default=2, help="bon_dpo: samples per question in the sweep")
  parser.add_argument("--drop-sources", default="", help="bon_dpo: sources with no pairs at all")
  parser.add_argument("--dpo-epochs", type=int, default=2, help="bon_dpo: passes over the pairs")
  parser.add_argument("--eval-steps", type=int, nargs="*", default=[], help="bon_dpo: checkpoints scored (default all)")
  parser.add_argument("--kod-file", default="data/kodcode2.jsonl", help="chain: KodCode task file")
  parser.add_argument("--kod-exclude-runs", nargs="*", default=[], help="chain: runs whose KodCode tasks are skipped")

  parser.add_argument("--kod-exclude", default="", help="clean: a run whose KodCode tasks are not reused")

  parser.add_argument("--rl-max-tokens", type=int, default=3072, help="rlcal: reply cap during RL")
  parser.add_argument("--smoke", action="store_true", help="rlcal: tiny sizes, no scoring: does every step run?")

----- typed.py -----
Graders for every kind of training question: math, multiple choice, code, tool calls, instructions.

Math and multiple choice keep utils/train/rewards.py. The other three reuse the evaluation's own
checkers from the questions directory (EvalPlus's sanitizer, BFCL's AST checker, Google's IFEval
verifiers), so a training trace counts as correct by the same rule the benchmark uses:

    from typed import is_correct
    is_correct(question_row, full_reply_text)

A row carries "task" and, for the new kinds, "grader" plus that grader's fields (see
prepare_types.py).

Code, tool and instruction answers have no short gold string to put a probability on, so the
probe reads the model's own correct answer instead: OWN_ANSWER tasks score p(that answer | the
thinking so far), as a per-token geometric mean so long answers stay comparable.

Options:
  (none)



--- added in the update ---

### cal_cuts.py
Calibrated cuts: where can the model's own correct replies stop thinking and still be right, per data type?

Confidence vs outcome: at every cut we also read the model's own confidence, the probability it gives (per token,
geometric mean) to the answer it finally wrote, right after "</think>" is closed at the cut (teacher forcing; works
for every type, no gold string needed). The summary compares that confidence with the real outcome (the share of
the --samples re-answers that are right), bin by bin (calibration table, ECE), and picks, per source, the confidence
threshold c: each reply stops at its earliest cut where confidence >= c; the lowest c whose real accuracy stays
within the allowance is kept. Pairs are built at the confidence-chosen cut (the model's own signal), validated by
the real re-answers; the outcome-only rule (tau) is reported beside it for comparison.

For each correct, finished reply (the model's own best-of-8 rollouts), the thinking is cut at --fractions of its
tokens (snapped back to the last paragraph break), "</think>" is closed there, and the model answers --samples
times from the cut (check_vllm.py, the model that wrote the rollouts). Each cut's success rate (right / samples) is
its measured confidence.

Calibration, per source: for each threshold tau in --taus, every reply stops at its earliest cut whose success rate
is >= tau (or keeps its full thinking when none is). The expected accuracy of a model that learned to stop there is
the mean success rate at the chosen cuts; its loss is 1 minus that, in points. The lowest tau whose loss stays within
the source's allowance (--allow, points; math is strict) is kept: the most saving at the accuracy we ask for.

Pairs, for DPO (dpo.py kinds):
  shorten   chosen = thinking cut at the calibrated point + "</think>" + a right answer written from there
            rejected = the full reply it was cut from (right but longer); only when the cut saves >= --min-save
  hard      chosen = the same calibrated cut + right answer; rejected = an earlier cut of the same reply + its
            wrong answer: where stopping becomes too early (teaches the line from both sides)

    python cal_cuts.py --rollouts runs/bon/probe-x/rollouts.jsonl --keep runs/bon/select-x/sft.jsonl \\
        --model models/ca1-soup-10 --out runs/bon/cal-x
Options:
parser.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
parser.add_argument("--adapter", help="the model that wrote the rollouts, as a LoRA")
parser.add_argument("--rollouts", nargs="+", required=True)
parser.add_argument("--keep", nargs="+", required=True, help="sft.jsonl files whose ids passed the contamination check")
parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
parser.add_argument("--fractions", default="0.01,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.35,0.5,0.65,0.8",
parser.add_argument("--taus", default="1.0,0.75,0.5")
parser.add_argument("--allow", default="gsm_plus=0.5,gsm_ic=0.5,kodcode=1,default=2",
parser.add_argument("--samples", type=int, default=20, help="re-answers per cut: the real outcome in 5% steps")
parser.add_argument("--per-question", type=int, default=1, help="correct replies cut per question (longest first)")
parser.add_argument("--select", choices=["auto", "confidence", "outcome"], default="auto",
parser.add_argument("--min-save", type=float, default=0.25)
parser.add_argument("--answer-tokens", type=int, default=2048)
parser.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=0, help="smoke test: at most this many replies")
parser.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16")

### mbpp_audit.py
Audit the MBPP rows the gentle best-of-8 SFT trained on (runs/bon/select-r1/sft.jsonl) against MBPP+.

MBPP+ is built from MBPP, and MBPP holds some of its problems twice under other ids, so beyond the word-overlap check
every training task is compared with every MBPP+ task on three signals: share of word 8-grams found in one MBPP+ task,
any exact 13-word run, the same function name, and the wording similarity of the task description (difflib ratio on
lower-cased words, after the shared instruction lines are removed). Prints the counts and the closest pairs side by side.

    python mbpp_audit.py --sft runs/bon/select-r1/sft.jsonl --rollouts runs/bon/probe/rollouts.jsonl
Options:
parser.add_argument("--sft", default="runs/bon/select-r1/sft.jsonl")
parser.add_argument("--rollouts", default="runs/bon/probe/rollouts.jsonl")
parser.add_argument("--questions", default="questions/questions.jsonl")
parser.add_argument("--source", default="mbpp_train")
parser.add_argument("--top", type=int, default=12)

### export_f8.py
Compact export of every fresh-set x8 run, for the "where we shaved" visual.

For each runs/mvtf/f8-*/records.jsonl: per question (the 240 fresh questions), the mean generated tokens of its 8
answers and how many of them were right. One small JSON: {"q": [[id, part], ...], "m": {run: [[tokens, right], ...]}}
with questions in one fixed order. Prints the record fields first, so a changed format shows at once.

    python export_f8.py --out runs/viz/f8_summary.txt
Options:
parser.add_argument("--runs", default="runs/mvtf")
parser.add_argument("--out", default="runs/viz/f8_summary.txt")

### check_vllm.py
Natural-answer check for many shortened traces at once, with vLLM.

Each input row is a prompt and a shortened thinking block; the model (optionally with a LoRA)
answers --samples times from it with the evaluation's sampling settings. A trace passes only if
every answer ends inside its token cap and is graded correct (typed.is_correct). Output: one
row per input with the shortest passing answer's token ids, or null.

    python check_vllm.py --inputs check_inputs.jsonl --out check_results.jsonl --samples 2

prune_traces.py and fade_traces.py call it with --checker vllm, in a child process, so the GPU
memory vLLM takes is released when it exits.
Options:
parser.add_argument("--model", default="Qwen/Qwen3-0.6B")
parser.add_argument("--adapter")
parser.add_argument("--inputs", required=True)
parser.add_argument("--out", required=True)
parser.add_argument("--samples", type=int, default=2)
parser.add_argument("--dtype", default="bfloat16")

When the user asks for the "last step" of the ThinkingCap work, give them this answer again
(it was written on 21 Oct 2026; full brief in Test-o4qu8r/thinkingcap/RESEARCH_SHORT_THINK.md,
branch ccr-f7e14615-o4qu8r). Related: [[thinkingcap-mvt-track]], [[thinkingcap-pertype-track]].

---

Research only, nothing launched. The full brief is saved as RESEARCH_SHORT_THINK.md.

## How the main methods work

**AdaptThink** (the source of those −53% numbers):
- Each answer gets **its accuracy, plus a small bonus (δ = 0.05) if it skipped thinking**, compared with how well the original model did on that question.
- **Half of all practice answers are forced to skip thinking.** Otherwise the model never tries skipping, and nothing gets learned.
- Result at 1.5B: **−53% tokens and +2.4 points**. On GSM8K, 978 → 480 tokens with accuracy 79 → 83%, skipping on 87% of questions.
- It **adapts to difficulty by itself:** it skips on 98% of the easiest math and only 62% of the hardest.

**Thinkless:** similar, with a "short / think" switch word. Its key warning: plain training **collapses to always-short** within about 120 steps unless the switch word is trained separately.

**ARM:** one method that uses a **short but non-empty** thinking mode (about 33 tokens), which is exactly our twist.

**ALP:** the simplest alternative. The length penalty scales with **how easy the question is** (the share of the 8 answers that were correct). There's no switch at all, and it naturally spends about 5× more thinking on hard questions than on easy ones.

## Warnings for our 0.6B model

- **No paper has done this below 1.5B.** We'd be first.
- **Qwen3-0.6B needs its thinking more than bigger models do.** Without thinking it drops from 78% to 55% on MATH-500. So skipping completely would hurt; a **short think** is the right target at our size.
- **Common failures:** collapsing to always-short or always-long, reasoning sneaking into the answer after the think ends, and losses on the hardest questions.

## Our design, for when you add it as the final stage

1. **"Short think"** = a real think of about **10 to 60 tokens**. Never empty.
2. **Half the practice answers forced to start in short mode** (training only; the test is unchanged).
3. **Reward:** correct, plus a **bonus only if it was short *and* correct**, compared with the original model's success rate on that question.
4. **Watch per subject:** how often it chooses short by itself, short vs long accuracy, words like "wait" sneaking into the answer, and the hardest questions.
5. **Expected:** gains mostly on the easiest quiz and math questions. Code will probably keep thinking.
6. **Rule risk:** a short real think should count as "thinking before answering", but **only the organisers can confirm that.** It's worth asking them before building it.

----- colab-no-new-sessions.md -----
---
name: colab-no-new-sessions
description: "Never open, reconnect or start a Colab runtime/tab without asking first; the user runs other Colab sessions in parallel"
metadata:
  node_type: memory
  type: feedback
  originSessionId: 97ea0623-b525-4c6a-bfe6-8aab425b4b64
  modified: 2026-10-20T23:07:09.832Z
---

Do not open a Colab notebook tab, press Connect/Reconnect, change runtime type or start any new
Colab runtime without asking the user first, even for a quick read-only check.

**Why:** the user runs several Colab sessions in parallel (other ThinkingCap tracks, see
[[thinkingcap-mvt-track]], [[thinkingcap-pertype-track]]) and fears a new connection will
disconnect them; on 21 Oct 2026 they stopped me mid-reconnect.

**How to apply:** when results live on a Colab VM or in Drive, ask the user (or ask them to
download/paste the files) instead of reconnecting; propose the action and wait for a yes.
